Lemma 27
.

[
12
]

Let
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}
be the principal character modulo
q
. Then we have

L(s,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})=\zeta(s)\prod_{p|q}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{s}}\right)

and

L(s,\mathrm{sym}^{2n}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})=L(s,\mathrm{sym}^{2n}f)\prod_{p|q}\prod_{0\leq j\leq 2n}\left(1-\frac{\alpha_{f}^{2n-2j}(p)}{p^{s}}\right)

for all integers
n\geq 1
and

\prod_{p|q}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{s}}\right)\ll q^{\epsilon},

\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|q\end{subarray}}\prod_{0\leq j\leq 2n}\left(1-\frac{\alpha_{f}^{2n-2j}(p)}{p^{s}}\right)\ll q^{\epsilon},

for
\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon<\Re(s)<1+\epsilon
.

Lemma 28
.

[
12
]

Let
\textstyle\chi
be a non-primitive character modulo q and
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}^{*}
be a primitive character modulo
q_{1}(\neq q)
induced by
\textstyle\chi
. Then we have

L(s,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}})=L(s,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}^{*})\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|q\\
p\nmid q_{1}\end{subarray}}\left(1-\frac{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}^{*}(p)}{p^{s}}\right),

L(s,\mathrm{sym}^{2n}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}})=L(s,\mathrm{sym}^{2n}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}^{*})\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|q\\
p\nmid q_{1}\end{subarray}}\prod_{0\leq j\leq 2n}\left(1-\frac{\alpha_{f}^{2n-2j}(p){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}^{*}(p)}{p^{s}}\right),

for all integers
n\geq 1
and

\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|q\\
p\nmid q_{1}\end{subarray}}\left(1-\frac{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}^{*}(p)}{p^{s}}\right)\ll q^{\epsilon},

\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|q\\
p\nmid q_{1}\end{subarray}}\prod_{0\leq j\leq 2n}\left(1-\frac{\alpha_{f}^{2n-2j}(p){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}^{*}(p)}{p^{s}}\right)\ll q^{\epsilon},

for
\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon<\Re(s)<1+\epsilon
.

Lemma 29
.

Let
\textstyle\chi
be any primitive character modulo
q
. Then for
q\ll T^{2}
, we have

(42)

\displaystyle L(\sigma+iT,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}})\ll(q(1+|T|))^{\mathrm{max}\{\frac{1}{3}(1-\sigma),0\}+\epsilon}

holds uniformly for
\frac{1}{2}\leq\sigma\leq 2
; and

(43)

\displaystyle\int_{1}^{T}|L(\sigma+iT,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}})|^{4}\ll(qT)^{2(1-\sigma)+\epsilon}

uniformly for
\frac{1}{2}\leq\sigma\leq 1+\epsilon
and
T\geq 1
.

Proof.

The first result follows from
[
5
]
and the second follows from
[
10
]
.
∎

Lemma 30
.

For any
\frac{1}{2}\leq\sigma\leq 2
and
T\geq 2
, we have

(44)

\displaystyle\int_{1}^{T}\left|\zeta\left(\sigma+it\right)\right|^{4}dt\ll T^{1+\epsilon},

and

(45)

\displaystyle\zeta(\sigma+it)\ll_{\epsilon}(1+|t|)^{\max\{\frac{13}{42}(1-\sigma),0\}+\epsilon},

uniformly for
\frac{1}{2}\leq\sigma\leq 1+\epsilon
, and
|t|\geq 1
.

Proof.

For the proof of (
44
), see
[
11
]
, page-
148
and (
45
) is due to Bourgain, for instance, see
[
1
]
.
∎

Lemma 31
.

Let
f\in S_{\kappa}
, and
\textstyle\chi
be a primitive character modulo
q
. Then for
q\ll T^{2}
, we have

(46)

\displaystyle L(\sigma+iT,\mathrm{sym}^{2}f)\ll(1+|T|)^{\mathrm{max}\{\frac{6}{5}(1-\sigma),0\}+\epsilon}

and

(47)

\displaystyle L(\sigma+iT,\mathrm{sym}^{2}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}})\ll(q(1+|T|))^{\mathrm{max}\{\frac{67}{46}(1-\sigma),0\}+\epsilon}

uniformly for
\frac{1}{2}\leq\sigma\leq 2
and
|T|\geq 1
;

(48)

\displaystyle\int_{1}^{T}|L(\sigma+iT,\mathrm{sym}^{2}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}})|^{4}dt\ll(qT)^{6(1-\sigma)+\epsilon}

uniformly for
\frac{1}{2}\leq\sigma\leq 1+\epsilon
and
T\geq 1
.

Proof.

(
46
) and (
47
) follow from Phragmén-Lindelöf convexity principle and the work of Lin, Nunes
and Qi
[
9
]
and Huang
[
6
]
respectively. (
48
) follows from Perelli
[
10
]
.
∎

Lemma 32
.

Let
\textstyle\chi
be a primitive character modulo
q
and
\mathfrak{L}_{m,n}^{d}(s,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}})
be a general
L
-function of degree
2A
. For any
\epsilon>0
, we have

(49)

\displaystyle\int_{T}^{2T}\left|\mathfrak{L}_{m,n}^{d}(\sigma+it,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}})\right|^{2}dt\ll(qT)^{2A(1-\sigma)+\epsilon},

uniformly for
\frac{1}{2}\leq\sigma\leq 1+\epsilon
, and
T\geq 1
. Also,

(50)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}_{m,n}^{d}(\sigma+it,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}})\ll(q(1+|t|))^{\max\{A(1-\sigma),0\}+\epsilon},

uniformly for
-\epsilon\leq\sigma\leq 1+\epsilon
.

Proof.

For the proof of (
49
) and (
50
), see
[
8
]
.
∎

Lemma 33
.

Let
f:\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
, and
T>1
. Then

(51)

\displaystyle\int_{1}^{T}\frac{|f(t)|}{t}dt\ll\log T\sup_{1\leq T_{1}\leq T}\frac{1}{T_{1}}\int_{T_{1}}^{2T_{1}}|f(t)|dt.

Lemma 34
.

Let
f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}_{>0}
satisfy

f(x)=Dx^{A}+O(x^{B})

as
x\to\infty
, where
A,B\in\mathbb{R}
,
D>0
, and
A>B
.
Then there exists
X_{0}>0
such that for all
x>X_{0}
,

f(x)\geq\frac{D}{2}x^{A}.

Lemma 35
.

Let
j\geq 2
be a fixed integer. For any fixed
\epsilon>0
and all sufficiently large
x
, we have

(52)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq x\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)r_{2}(n)=\begin{cases}O\left(x^{1-\frac{1}{j+1}+\epsilon}q^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\right)\quad\text{ when }{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}={\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}\text{ or }{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}={\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0},\\
O\left(x^{1-\frac{1}{j+1}+\epsilon}q^{1+\epsilon}\right)\quad\text{ when }{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}\neq{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}\text{ and }{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}\neq{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}.\end{cases}

Proof.

Let
\textstyle\chi
be a Dirichlet character modulo
q
. We estimate the term
\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq x\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}(n)l_{1}(n)
. As in the previous paper, We begin by applying Perron’s formula to
F_{j}^{(1)}(s)
with
\eta=1+\epsilon
, and
10\leq T\leq x
. Thus, we have,

(53)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq x\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}(n)l_{1}(n)

\displaystyle=\int_{1+\epsilon-iT}^{1+\epsilon+iT}F_{j}^{(1)}(s,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})\frac{x^{s}}{s}ds+O\left(\frac{x^{1+\epsilon}}{T}\right).

After moving the line of integration to
\Re(s)=\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon
, by Cauchy’s residue theorem, there are no poles due to the Lemma
11
.
So we obtain,

(54)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq x\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n)l_{1}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}(n)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\left\{\int_{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon-iT}^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon+iT}+\int_{1+\epsilon-iT}^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon-iT}+\int_{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon+iT}^{1+\epsilon+iT}\right\}F_{j}^{(1)}(s,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})\frac{x^{s}}{s}ds

(55)

\displaystyle\quad+O\left(\frac{x^{1+\epsilon}}{T}\right)

(56)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi i}(J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3})+O\left(\frac{x^{1+\epsilon}}{T}\right).\quad\text{(say)}

Contribution of horizontal line integrals (
J_{2}
and
J_{3}
) in absolute value (using Lemmas
11
and
32
) is

(57)

\displaystyle|J_{2}+J_{3}|

\displaystyle=\left|\left(\int_{1+\epsilon-iT}^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon-iT}+\int_{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon+iT}^{1+\epsilon+iT}\right)F_{j}^{(1)}(s,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})\frac{x^{s}}{s}\right|

(58)

\displaystyle\ll\left(\int_{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}^{1+\epsilon}+\int_{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}^{1+\epsilon}\right)\frac{|L(\sigma+iT,\mathrm{sym}^{j}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})L(\sigma+iT,\mathrm{sym}^{j}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})|}{T}x^{\sigma}d\sigma,

\displaystyle J_{2}+J_{3}

\displaystyle\ll\int_{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}^{1+\epsilon}\frac{|T|^{\frac{j+1}{2}(1-\sigma)+\epsilon}|qT|^{\frac{j+1}{2}(1-\sigma)+\epsilon}q^{\epsilon}}{T}x^{\sigma}d\sigma\quad\text{ (using~\ref{lem:genLbound}) }

\displaystyle\ll\frac{1}{T}\max_{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon<\sigma<1+\epsilon}\left(x^{\sigma}q^{\frac{j+1}{2}(1-\sigma)+\epsilon}T^{(j+1)(1-\sigma)+\epsilon}\right).

Clearly,
x^{\sigma}q^{\frac{j+1}{2}(1-\sigma)+\epsilon}T^{(j+1)(1-\sigma)+\epsilon}
is a monotonic function, so the maximum occurs at the endpoints of the interval. We take values at both extreme points of the interval
[\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon,1+\epsilon]
. So

\displaystyle J_{2}+J_{3}

\displaystyle\ll\frac{1}{T}\left(x^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}q^{\frac{j+1}{4}+\epsilon}T^{\frac{(j+1)}{2}+\epsilon)}\right)+\frac{1}{T}\left(x^{1+\epsilon}(qT)^{\epsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\ll\frac{x^{1+\epsilon}}{T}q^{\epsilon}+x^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}q^{\frac{j+1}{4}+\epsilon}T^{\frac{j+1}{2}-1+\epsilon}.

Now contribution of vertical line integral
J_{1}
in absolute value is

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon-iT}^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon+iT}F_{j}^{(1)}(s,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})\frac{x^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it}}{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it}ds

\displaystyle=x^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\left(\int_{0\leq|t|\leq 1}+\int_{1\leq|t|\leq T}\right)F_{j}^{(1)}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}\right)\frac{x^{it}}{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it}idt

\displaystyle=I_{1}+I_{2}.

Now using the Lemma
32
, we have

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle\ll x^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\int_{1}^{T}|L(\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it,\mathrm{sym}^{j}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})L(\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it,\mathrm{sym}^{j}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})|\frac{1}{t}dt

\displaystyle\ll x^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\log T\text{ }\sup_{1\leq T_{1}\leq T}\frac{1}{T_{1}}\int_{T_{1}}^{2T_{1}}|L(\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it,\mathrm{sym}^{j}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})L(\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it,\mathrm{sym}^{j}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})|dt

\displaystyle\ll x^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\log T\text{ }\sup_{1\leq T_{1}\leq T}\frac{1}{T_{1}}\left(\int_{T_{1}}^{2T_{1}}|L(\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it,\mathrm{sym}^{j}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})|^{2}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\int_{T_{1}}^{2T_{1}}|L(\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it,\mathrm{sym}^{j}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})|^{2}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\ll x^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\sup_{1\leq T_{1}\leq T}\frac{1}{T_{1}}\left(T_{1}^{\max\{(j+1)(1-\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon),0\}+\epsilon})^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\left((qT_{1})^{\max\{(j+1)(1-\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon),0\}+\epsilon})^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)q^{\epsilon}\quad\text{(using~\ref{lem:genLbound})}

\displaystyle\ll x^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}q^{\frac{j+1}{4}+\epsilon}T^{\frac{j+1}{2}-1+\epsilon}.

The first integral gives

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=x^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\int_{0\leq|t|\leq 1}F_{j}^{(1)}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}\right)\frac{x^{it}}{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it}dt.

The above integration is finite. If not, then the left-hand side of (
54
) would be infinite. As the other integral is finite, this is a contradiction. So,

\displaystyle I_{1}\ll x^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}.

Combining
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
, we have

(59)

\displaystyle J_{1}\ll x^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}+x^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}q^{\frac{j+1}{4}}T^{\frac{j+1}{2}-1+\epsilon}.

Thus we have

(60)

\displaystyle J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3}\ll\frac{x^{1+\epsilon}}{T}q^{\epsilon}+x^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}q^{\frac{j+1}{4}+\epsilon}T^{\frac{j+1}{2}-1+\epsilon}.

So
\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq x\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}(n)l_{1}(n)=O\left(\frac{x^{1+\epsilon}}{T}q^{\epsilon}+x^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}q^{\frac{j+1}{4}+\epsilon}T^{\frac{j+1}{2}-1+\epsilon}\right)
.

Now put
T=\frac{x^{\frac{1}{j+1}}}{q^{\frac{1}{2}}}
, then

(61)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq x\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}(n)l_{1}(n)=O\left(x^{1-\frac{1}{j+1}+\epsilon}q^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\right).

Note that the same bound of
\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq x\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)l_{1}(n)
happens when
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}={\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}
,
and after doing the same calculation for
\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq x\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)l_{1}(n)
, where
\textstyle\chi
is any primitive or non primitive Dirichlet charachter modulo
q
and
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}\neq{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}
, we have

(62)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq x\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)l_{1}(n)=O\left(x^{1-\frac{1}{j+1}+\epsilon}q^{1+\epsilon}\right).

∎

Lemma 36
.

Let
j\geq 2
be a fixed integer. For any fixed
\epsilon>0
and all sufficiently large
x
, we have

(63)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq x\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)r_{m}(n)=\begin{cases}O\left(x^{\frac{m}{2}-\frac{2}{j+1}+\epsilon}q^{\epsilon}\right)\quad\text{ when }{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}={\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0},\\
O\left(x^{\frac{m}{2}-\frac{2}{j+3}+\epsilon}q^{\frac{j+1}{j+3}+\epsilon}\right)\quad\text{ when }{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}\neq{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}.\end{cases}

Proof.

We estimate the sum
\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq x\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}(n)l_{2}(n)
.

Now By Perron’s formula, we have

(64)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq x\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}(n)l_{2}(n)

\displaystyle=\int_{2+\epsilon-iT}^{2+\epsilon+iT}F_{j}^{(2)}(s,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})\frac{x^{s}}{s}ds+O\left(\frac{x^{2+\epsilon}}{T}\right).

We move the line of integration to
\Re(s)=\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon
and by the Cauchy residue theorem, we get that there exists no pole in the area of integration due to the Lemma
12
.

(65)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq x\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}(n)l_{2}(n)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\left\{\int_{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon-iT}^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon+iT}+\int_{2+\epsilon-iT}^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon-iT}+\int_{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon+iT}^{2+\epsilon+iT}\right\}F_{j}^{(2)}(s,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})\frac{x^{s}}{s}ds

(66)

\displaystyle\quad+O\left(\frac{x^{2+\epsilon}}{T}\right)

(67)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi i}(J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3})+O\left(\frac{x^{2+\epsilon}}{T}\right).\quad\text{(say)}

Contribution of horizontal line integrals (
J_{2}
and
J_{3}
) in absolute value (using Lemmas
12
,
27
and
32
) is

\displaystyle|J_{2}+J_{3}|

\displaystyle=\left|\left(\int_{2+\epsilon-iT}^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon-iT}+\int_{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon+iT}^{2+\epsilon+iT}\right)F_{j}^{(2)}(s,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})\frac{x^{s}}{s}\right|

\displaystyle\ll\left(\int_{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}^{2+\epsilon}+\int_{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}^{2+\epsilon}\right)\frac{|L(\sigma+iT-1,\mathrm{sym}^{j}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})|}{T}x^{\sigma}d\sigma.

The above inequality happens because
F_{j}^{(2)}(s,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})=G_{j}^{(2)}(s,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})H_{j}^{(2)}(s,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})
and
H_{j}^{(2)}(s,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})\ll 1
for
\Re(s)>\frac{3}{2}.
So
F_{j}^{(2)}(s,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})\ll G_{j}^{(2)}(s,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})=L(s-1,\mathrm{sym}^{j}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})L(s,\mathrm{sym}^{j}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})
. Now
L(s,\mathrm{sym}^{j}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})
is absolutely convergent for
\Re(s)>1
. So
L(s,\mathrm{sym}^{j}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})\ll 1
in
\Re(s)>\frac{3}{2}
.

\displaystyle J_{2}+J_{3}

\displaystyle\ll\int_{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}^{2+\epsilon}\frac{|L(\sigma-1+iT,\mathrm{sym}^{j}f)|}{T}x^{\sigma}q^{\epsilon}d\sigma\quad(\text{ by Lemma~\ref{lem:PrincipalChar}})

\displaystyle\ll\int_{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}^{1+\epsilon}\frac{|L(\sigma+iT,\mathrm{sym}^{j}f)|}{T}x^{\sigma+1}q^{\epsilon}d\sigma

\displaystyle\ll\int_{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}^{1+\epsilon}\frac{|T|^{\frac{j+1}{2}(1-\sigma)+\epsilon}}{T}x^{\sigma+1}q^{\epsilon}d\sigma\quad\text{ (using~\ref{lem:genLbound})}

\displaystyle\ll\frac{x}{T}q^{\epsilon}\max_{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon<\sigma<1+\epsilon}\left(x^{\sigma}T^{\frac{j+1}{2}(1-\sigma)+\epsilon}\right).

Clearly,
x^{\sigma}T^{\frac{j+1}{2}(1-\sigma)+\epsilon}
is a monotonic function, so the maximum occurs at the endpoints of the interval. We take values at both extreme points of the interval
[\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon,1+\epsilon]
. So

\displaystyle J_{2}+J_{3}

\displaystyle\ll\frac{x}{T}q^{\epsilon}\left(x^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}T^{\frac{j+1}{2}(1-\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)+\epsilon}\right)+\frac{x}{T}q^{\epsilon}\left(x^{1+\epsilon}T^{\frac{j+1}{2}(1-1-\epsilon)+\epsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\ll\frac{x^{2+\epsilon}}{T}q^{\epsilon}+x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}T^{\frac{j+1}{4}-1+\epsilon}q^{\epsilon}.

Now contribution of vertical line integral
J_{1}
in absolute value is

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon-iT}^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon+iT}F_{j}^{(2)}(s,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})\frac{x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon+it}}{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon+it}ds

\displaystyle=(x+1)^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}\left(\int_{0\leq|t|\leq 1}+\int_{1\leq|t|\leq T}\right)F_{j}^{(2)}\left(\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon+it,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}\right)\frac{x^{it}}{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon+it}idt

\displaystyle=I_{1}+I_{2}.

Now

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle\ll x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}q^{\epsilon}\int_{1}^{T}|L(\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it,\mathrm{sym}^{j}f)|\frac{1}{t}dt\quad(\text{ by Lemma }~\ref{lem:PrincipalChar}\text{ and }~\ref{lem:F2})

\displaystyle\ll x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}q^{\epsilon}\log T\text{ }\sup_{1\leq T_{1}\leq T}\frac{1}{T_{1}}\int_{T_{1}}^{2T_{1}}|L(\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it,\mathrm{sym}^{j}f)|dt(\text{ by Lemma }~\ref{lem:int}

\displaystyle\ll x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}q^{\epsilon}\log T\text{ }\sup_{1\leq T_{1}\leq T}\frac{1}{T_{1}}\left(\int_{T_{1}}^{2T_{1}}|L(\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it,\mathrm{sym}^{j}f)|^{2}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{T_{1}}^{2T_{1}}1dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\ll x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}q^{\epsilon}\sup_{1\leq T_{1}\leq T}\frac{1}{T_{1}}(T_{1}^{\max\{(j+1)(1-\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon),0\}+\epsilon})^{\frac{1}{2}}T_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\quad\text{(using~\ref{lem:genLbound})}

\displaystyle\ll x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}T^{\frac{j+1}{4}-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}q^{\epsilon}.

The first integral gives

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}\int_{0\leq|t|\leq 1}F_{j}^{(2)}\left(\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon+it,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}\right)\frac{x^{it}}{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon+it}dt.

The above integration is finite. If not, then
\sum_{1}
would be infinite. As the other integral is finite, this is a contradiction. So,

\displaystyle I_{1}\ll x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}.

Combining
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
, we have

(68)

\displaystyle J_{1}\ll x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}+x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}T^{\frac{j+1}{4}-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}q^{\epsilon}.

Thus we have

(69)

\displaystyle J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3}\ll\frac{x^{2+\epsilon}}{T}q^{\epsilon}+x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}T^{\frac{j+1}{4}-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}q^{\epsilon}.

We have

(70)

\displaystyle\sum_{n\leq x}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n)l_{2}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}(n)=O\left(\frac{x^{2+\epsilon}}{T}q^{\epsilon}+x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}T^{\frac{j+1}{4}-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}q^{\epsilon}\right).

Put
T=x^{\frac{2}{j+3}}
, then

(71)

\displaystyle\sum_{n\leq x}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n)l_{2}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}(n)=O\left(x^{2-\frac{2}{j+3}+\epsilon}q^{\epsilon}\right).

Now for
\sum_{n\leq x}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n)l_{2}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)
, where
\textstyle\chi
is a primitive Dirichlet character modulo
q
, using the lemmas
28
,
32
, we have

(72)

\displaystyle J_{2}+J_{3}

\displaystyle\ll\int_{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}^{2+\epsilon}\frac{|L(\sigma-1+iT,\mathrm{sym}^{j}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}})|}{T}x^{\sigma}d\sigma

(73)

\displaystyle\ll\frac{x^{2+\epsilon}}{T}q^{\epsilon}+x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}q^{\frac{j+1}{4}+\epsilon}T^{\frac{j+1}{4}-1+\epsilon}q^{\epsilon}

and

(74)

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle\ll\left(\int_{0}^{1}+\int_{1}^{T}\right)\left|L\left(\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it,\mathrm{sym}^{j}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}\right)\right|\frac{dt}{t}

(75)

\displaystyle\ll x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}+x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}q^{\frac{j+1}{4}}T^{\frac{j+1}{4}-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}.

Thus

(76)

\displaystyle J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3}\ll\frac{x^{2+\epsilon}}{T}q^{\epsilon}+x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}q^{\frac{j+1}{4}+\epsilon}T^{\frac{j+1}{4}-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}.

Thus

(77)

\displaystyle\sum_{n\leq x}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n)l_{2}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)=O\left(\frac{x^{2+\epsilon}}{T}q^{\epsilon}+x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}q^{\frac{j+1}{4}+\epsilon}T^{\frac{j+1}{4}-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\right).

Put
T=\frac{x^{\frac{2}{j+3}}}{q^{\frac{j+1}{j+3}}}
. Then

(78)

\displaystyle\sum_{n\leq x}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n)l_{2}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)=O\left(x^{2-\frac{2}{j+3}+\epsilon}q^{\frac{j+1}{j+3}+\epsilon}\right),

for any non primitive Dirichlet character modulo
q
.

Now for
m=6
, We have,

(79)

\displaystyle\sum_{n\leq x}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n)l_{3}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}(n)=O\left(\frac{x^{3+\epsilon}}{T}q^{\epsilon}+x^{\frac{5}{2}+\epsilon}q^{\epsilon}T^{\frac{j+1}{4}-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\right)

and

(80)

\displaystyle\sum_{n\leq x}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n)v_{3}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}(n)=O\left(\frac{x^{3+\epsilon}}{T}q^{\epsilon}+x^{\frac{5}{2}+\epsilon}q^{\epsilon}T^{\frac{j+1}{4}-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\right).

From (
22
), we have

(81)

\displaystyle\sum_{n\leq x}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n)r_{6}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}(n)=O\left(\frac{x^{3+\epsilon}}{T}q^{\epsilon}+x^{\frac{5}{2}+\epsilon}q^{\epsilon}T^{\frac{j+1}{4}-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\right).

Put
T=x^{\frac{2}{j+3}}
, then

(82)

\displaystyle\sum_{n\leq x}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n)r_{6}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}(n)=O\left(x^{3-\frac{2}{j+3}+\epsilon}q^{\epsilon}\right).

For
\chi\neq\chi_{0}
,

(83)

\displaystyle\sum_{n\leq x}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n)v_{3}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)=O\left(\frac{x^{3+\epsilon}}{T}q^{\epsilon}+x^{\frac{5}{2}+\epsilon}q^{\frac{j+1}{4}+\epsilon}T^{\frac{j+1}{4}-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\right),

and

(84)

\displaystyle\sum_{n\leq x}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n)v_{3}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)=O\left(\frac{x^{3+\epsilon}}{T}q^{\epsilon}+x^{\frac{5}{2}+\epsilon}q^{\frac{j+1}{4}+\epsilon}T^{\frac{j+1}{4}-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\right)

and from (
22
), we have

(85)

\displaystyle\sum_{n\leq x}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n)r_{6}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)=O\left(\frac{x^{3+\epsilon}}{T}q^{\epsilon}+x^{\frac{5}{2}+\epsilon}q^{\frac{j+1}{4}+\epsilon}T^{\frac{j+1}{4}-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\right).

Put
T=\frac{x^{\frac{2}{j+1}}}{q^{\frac{j+1}{j+3}}}
. Then

(86)

\displaystyle\sum_{n\leq x}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n)r_{6}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)=O\left(x^{3-\frac{2}{j+3}+\epsilon}q^{\frac{j+1}{j+3}+\epsilon}\right),

for any non primitive Dirichlet character modulo
q
.

Following the above process, we will have the same bounds for
\sum_{n\leq x}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n)l_{\frac{m}{2}}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)
, for
m=6,8,10,12
and
\sum_{n\leq x}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n)v_{\frac{m}{2}}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)
for
m=6,10
.

For the sum of
10
squares, we have an additional term
\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)a_{n}
beside the terms
\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)l_{5}(n)
and
\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)v_{5}(n)
, as we can see from (
32
). We have from
[
4
]
that
a_{n}=O(n^{3})
. As shown in a parallel paper, we need only to compute the terms involving
l_{5}(n)
and
v_{5}(n)
.

For the sum of
12
squares, we have an additional term
\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)b_{n}
beside the term
\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)l_{6}(n)
,as we can see from (
34
) and we have from
[
4
]
that
b_{n}=O(n^{3}\log\log n)
. As shown in a parallel paper, we only need to calculate the term with
l_{6}(n)
.

Thus we have, using (
21
), (
23
) and (
25
), we have

(87)

\displaystyle\sum_{n\leq x}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n)r_{m}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}(n)=O\left(x^{\frac{m}{2}-\frac{2}{j+3}+\epsilon}q^{\epsilon}\right),

and

(88)

\displaystyle\sum_{n\leq x}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n)r_{m}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)

\displaystyle=O\left(x^{\frac{m}{2}-\frac{2}{j+3}+\epsilon}q^{\frac{j+1}{j+3}+\epsilon}\right).

∎

Lemma 37
.

Let
j\geq 2
be a fixed integer. For any fixed
\epsilon>0
and all sufficiently large
x
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq x\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)r_{2}(n)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}A\frac{\phi(q)}{q}x+O\left(x^{1-\frac{1}{(j+1)^{2}}+\epsilon}q^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\right),\quad\text{ when }{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}={\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}\text{ or }{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}={\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0},\\
O\left(x^{1-\frac{1}{(j+1)^{2}}+\epsilon}q^{1+\epsilon}\right),\quad\text{ when }{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}\neq{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}\text{ and }{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}\neq{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0},\end{cases}

where
A
is a constant.

Proof.

We consider the sum

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq x\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)l_{1}(n).

We begin by applying Perron’s formula to
\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq x\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)l_{1}(n)
with
\eta=1+\epsilon
, and
10\leq T\leq x
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{n\leq x}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)l_{1}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}(n)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\eta-iT}^{\eta+iT}F_{j}^{(*1)}(s,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})\frac{x^{s}}{s}ds+O\left(\frac{x^{1+\epsilon}}{T}\right).

We move the line of integration to
\Re(s)=\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon
, and by Cauchy’s residue theorem, there is only one simple pole at
s=1
due to the factor
L(s,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})
, we get from
F^{(*1)}_{j}(s)
in the Lemma
19
.
This contributes a residue, which is
\frac{\phi(q)}{q}c_{j,f}(1)(x+1)
, where

(89)

\displaystyle c_{j,f}(1)

\displaystyle=L(1,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})\prod_{n=1}^{j}L(1,\mathrm{sym}^{2n}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})L(1,\mathrm{sym}^{2n}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})H_{j}^{(*1)}(1,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}).

So, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{n\leq x}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)l_{1}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}(n)

\displaystyle=\frac{\phi(q)}{q}c_{j,f}(1)x

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2\pi i}\left\{\int_{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon-iT}^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon+iT}+\int_{1+\epsilon-iT}^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon-iT}+\int_{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon+iT}^{1+\epsilon+iT}\right\}F_{j}^{(*1)}(s,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})\frac{x^{s}}{s}ds+O\left(\frac{x^{1+\epsilon}}{T}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\phi(q)}{q}c_{j,f}(1)x+\frac{1}{2\pi i}(J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3})+O\left(\frac{x^{1+\epsilon}}{T}\right).\quad\text{(say)}

\displaystyle\ll\int_{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}^{1+\epsilon}\left|\zeta(\sigma+iT)L(\sigma+iT,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}^{*})\prod_{n=1}^{j}L(\sigma+iT,\mathrm{sym}^{2n}f)L(\sigma+iT,\mathrm{sym}^{2n}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}^{*})\right|\frac{1}{T}x^{\sigma}q^{\epsilon}d\sigma

\displaystyle\ll\frac{1}{T}\max_{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon\leq\sigma\leq 1+\epsilon}x^{\sigma}T^{(\frac{13}{42}+\frac{6}{5}+\sum_{2\leq n\leq j}\frac{2n+1}{2})(1-\sigma)+\epsilon}(qT)^{(\frac{1}{3}+\frac{67}{46}+\sum_{2\leq n\leq j}\frac{2n+1}{2})(1-\sigma)+\epsilon}

\displaystyle\ll\frac{1}{T}\max_{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon\leq\sigma\leq 1+\epsilon}x^{\sigma}q^{\left(\frac{(j+1)^{2}}{2}-\frac{29}{138}\right)(1-\sigma)+\epsilon}T^{\left((j+1)^{2}-\frac{564}{805}\right)(1-\sigma)+\epsilon}.

The above function involving
\sigma
is monotonic, so the maximum happens at the endpoints. We treat both boundary points as upper bounds.

\displaystyle J_{2}+J_{3}

\displaystyle\ll\frac{x^{1+\epsilon}}{T}q^{\epsilon}+x^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}q^{\frac{(j+1)^{2}}{4}-\frac{29}{276}+\epsilon}T^{\frac{(j+1)^{2}}{2}-\frac{1087}{805}+\epsilon}.

\displaystyle\ll\int_{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon-iT}^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon+iT}\left|\zeta(\tfrac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it)L(\tfrac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}^{*})\right|

\displaystyle\times\left|\prod_{n=1}^{j}L(\tfrac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it,\mathrm{sym}^{2n}f)L(\tfrac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it,\mathrm{sym}^{2n}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}^{*})\right|\frac{x^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}}{|t|}q^{\epsilon}dt

\displaystyle\times\left|\prod_{n=1}^{j}L(\tfrac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it,\mathrm{sym}^{2n}f)L(\tfrac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it,\mathrm{sym}^{2n}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}^{*})\right|\frac{1}{|t|}q^{\epsilon}dt

\displaystyle\ll x^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}+x^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}q^{\epsilon}\log T\text{ }\sup_{1\leq T_{1}\leq T}\frac{1}{T_{1}}\int_{T_{1}}^{2T_{1}}\left|\zeta(\tfrac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it)L(\tfrac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}^{*})\right|

For
\sum_{n\leq x}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)l_{1}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)

Proceeding as above, we have

(102)

\displaystyle\sum_{n\leq x}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)l_{1}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)=\frac{\phi(4q)}{4q}d_{j,f}(1)x+O\left(x^{1-\frac{1}{(j+1)^{2}}+\epsilon}q^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\right),

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}={\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}
.

, where

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}\neq{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq x\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)l_{1}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\eta-iT}^{\eta+iT}F_{j}^{(*1)}(s,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}})\frac{x^{s}}{s}ds+O\left(\frac{x^{1+\epsilon}}{T}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+O\left(\frac{x^{1+\epsilon}}{T}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi i}(J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3})+O\left(\frac{x^{1+\epsilon}}{T}\right).

After doing a similar calculation, we have the same bounds for

as in the previous case.

After doing a similar calculation, we have

(103)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq x\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)l_{1}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)=O\left(\frac{x^{1+\epsilon}}{T}q^{\epsilon}+x^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}q^{\frac{(j+1)^{2}}{2}+\epsilon}T^{\frac{(j+1)^{2}}{2}-1+\epsilon}\right).

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}\neq{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}

Lemma 38
.

Let
j\geq 2
be a fixed integer. For
m=4,8,12
and any fixed
\epsilon>0
and all sufficiently large
x
, we have

(105)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)r_{m}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)=\begin{cases}A\frac{\phi(q)}{q}x^{\frac{m}{2}}+O\left(x^{\frac{m}{2}-\frac{2}{(j+1)^{2}}+\epsilon}q^{\epsilon}\right),\quad\text{ when }{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}={\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0},\\
O\left(x^{\frac{m}{2}-\frac{2}{(j+1)^{2}}+\epsilon}q^{1+\epsilon}\right),\quad\text{ when }{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}\neq{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}.\end{cases}

Proof.

We first calculate
\sum_{n\leq x}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)l_{2}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}(n)
.

Using the Lemma
20
, Cauchy residue theorem,

(106)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)l_{2}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}(n)

\displaystyle=\frac{\phi(q)}{q}a_{j,f}(2)x^{2}+\frac{1}{2\pi i}\left\{\int_{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon-iT}^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon+iT}+\int_{2+\epsilon-iT}^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon-iT}+\int_{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon+iT}^{2+\epsilon+iT}\right\}F_{j}^{(*2)}(s,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})\frac{x^{s}}{s}ds

(107)

\displaystyle\quad+O\left(\frac{x^{2+\epsilon}}{T}\right)

(108)

\displaystyle=\frac{\phi(q)}{q}a_{j,f}(2)x^{2}+\frac{1}{2\pi i}(J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3})+O\left(\frac{x^{2+\epsilon}}{T}\right),\quad\text{(say)}

where

(109)

\displaystyle a_{j,f}(2)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}L(2,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})\prod_{n=1}^{j}L(2,\mathrm{sym}^{2n}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})L(1,\mathrm{sym}^{2n}f\times{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})H_{j}^{2}(2,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})

Now using the lemmas
20
,
27
,
30
,
31
,
32
, we have

(110)

\displaystyle J_{2}+J_{3}

\displaystyle\ll\left|\left(\int_{2+\epsilon-iT}^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon-iT}+\int_{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon+iT}^{2+\epsilon+iT}\right)F_{j}^{*2}(s,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})\frac{x^{s}}{s}\right|

(111)

\displaystyle\ll\int_{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}^{2+\epsilon}\left|\zeta(\sigma-1+iT)\prod_{n=1}^{j}L(\sigma+iT-1,\mathrm{sym}^{2n}f)\right|\frac{1}{T}x^{\sigma}q^{\epsilon}d\sigma

(112)

\displaystyle=\int_{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}^{1+\epsilon}\left|\zeta(\sigma+iT)\prod_{n=1}^{j}L(\sigma+iT,\mathrm{sym}^{2n}f)\right|\frac{x^{\sigma+1}}{T}q^{\epsilon}d\sigma

(113)

\displaystyle\ll\frac{x}{T}q^{\epsilon}\max_{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon\leq\sigma\leq 1+\epsilon}x^{\sigma}T^{(\frac{13}{42}+\frac{6}{5}+\sum_{2\leq n\leq j}\frac{2n+1}{2})(1-\sigma)+\epsilon}

(114)

\displaystyle\ll\frac{x}{T}q^{\epsilon}\max_{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon\leq\sigma\leq 1+\epsilon}x^{\sigma}T^{(\frac{(j+1)^{2}}{2}-\frac{103}{210})(1-\sigma)+\epsilon}.

The above function involving
\sigma
is monotonic, so the maximum happens at the extreme points. We take both boundary points as an upper bound.

\displaystyle J_{2}+J_{3}

\displaystyle\ll\frac{x}{T}q^{\epsilon}\left[x^{(1+\epsilon)}T^{(\frac{(j+1)^{2}}{2}-\frac{103}{210})(\epsilon)+\epsilon}+x^{(\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon)}T^{(\frac{(j+1)^{2}}{2}-\frac{103}{210})(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)+\epsilon}\right]

\displaystyle\ll\frac{x^{2+\epsilon}}{T}q^{\epsilon}+x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}T^{\frac{(j+1)^{2}}{4}-\frac{523}{420}+\epsilon}q^{\epsilon}.

Contribution of the left vertical line integral (
J_{1}
) in absolute value (using Lemmas
20
,
29
,
30
,
31
,
32
and Hölder’s inequality) is

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle\ll\int_{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon-iT}^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon+iT}\left|\zeta(\tfrac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it)\prod_{n=1}^{j}L(\tfrac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it,\mathrm{sym}^{2n}f)\right|\frac{x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}}{|t|}dt

\displaystyle\ll x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}+x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}\int_{1\leq|t|\leq T}\left|\zeta(\tfrac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it)\prod_{n=1}^{j}L(\tfrac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it,\mathrm{sym}^{2n}f)\right|\frac{1}{|t|}dt

\displaystyle\ll x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}+x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}\log T\text{ }\sup_{1\leq T_{1}\leq T}\frac{1}{T_{1}}\int_{T_{1}}^{2T_{1}}\left|\zeta(\tfrac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it)\prod_{n=1}^{j}L(\tfrac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it,\mathrm{sym}^{2n}f)\right|dt

\displaystyle=x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}+x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}I_{2},

where the bounds of
I_{2}
is given by as follows,

(115)

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle=q^{\epsilon}\log T\sup_{1\leq T_{1}\leq T}\frac{1}{T_{1}}\int_{T_{1}}^{2T_{1}}\left|\zeta(\tfrac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it)\prod_{n=1}^{j}L(\tfrac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it,\mathrm{sym}^{2n}f)\right|dt

(116)

\displaystyle\ll q^{\epsilon}T^{\epsilon}\sup_{1\leq T_{1}\leq T}\frac{1}{T_{1}}\left(\int_{T_{1}}^{2T_{1}}|\zeta(\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it)|^{4}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\int_{T_{1}}^{2T_{1}}|L(\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it,\mathrm{sym}^{2}f)|^{4}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}

(117)

\displaystyle\times\left(\int_{T_{1}}^{2T_{1}}\left|\prod_{n=2}^{j}L(\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon+it,\mathrm{sym}^{2n}f)\right|^{2}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(118)

\displaystyle\ll q^{\epsilon}T^{\frac{1}{4}+\epsilon+6(\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon)\frac{1}{4}+((j+1)^{2}-4)(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)\frac{1}{2}-1}=q^{\epsilon}T^{\frac{(j+1)^{2}}{4}-1+\epsilon}.

Thus, we have

(119)

\displaystyle J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3}\ll\frac{x^{2+\epsilon}}{T}q^{\epsilon}+x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}T^{\frac{(j+1)^{2}}{4}-1+\epsilon}q^{\epsilon}.

Thus

(120)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)l_{2}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}(n)=\frac{\phi(q)}{q}a_{j,f}(2)x^{2}+O\left(\frac{x^{2+\epsilon}}{T}q^{\epsilon}+x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}T^{\frac{(j+1)^{2}}{4}-1+\epsilon}q^{\epsilon}\right).

Now put
T=x^{\frac{2}{(j+1)^{2}}}
, then we have

(121)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)l_{2}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}(n)=\frac{\phi(q)}{q}a_{j,f}(2)x^{2}+O\left(x^{2-\frac{2}{(j+1)^{2}}+\epsilon}q^{\epsilon}\right).

Now for
\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)l_{2}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)
, where
\textstyle\chi
is a non principal but a primitive Dirichlet character modulo
q
, we have by using the Lemma
20
and by Cauchy residue theorem and by Perron’s formula, we have

(122)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)l_{2}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\left\{\int_{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon-iT}^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon+iT}+\int_{2+\epsilon-iT}^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon-iT}+\int_{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon+iT}^{2+\epsilon+iT}\right\}F_{j}^{(*2)}(s,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}})\frac{x^{s}}{s}ds

(123)

\displaystyle\quad+O\left(\frac{x^{2+\epsilon}}{T}\right)

(124)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi i}(J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3})+O\left(\frac{x^{2+\epsilon}}{T}\right).\quad\text{(say)}.

Proceeding as before, we have by using the Lemmas
20
,
28
,
29
,
31
and
32
), we have

(125)

\displaystyle J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3}\ll\frac{x^{2+\epsilon}}{T}q^{\epsilon}+x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}q^{{}^{\frac{(j+1)^{2}}{4}+\epsilon}}T^{\frac{(j+1)^{2}}{4}-1+\epsilon}.

If we put
T=\frac{x^{\frac{2}{(j+1)^{2}}}}{q}
, then we have

(126)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)l_{2}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)

\displaystyle=O\left(x^{2-\frac{2}{(j+1)^{2}}+\epsilon}q^{1+\epsilon}\right).

Similarly we have for
m=8,12
,

(127)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)l_{\frac{m}{2}}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}(n)=\frac{\phi(q)}{q}a_{j,f}(\frac{m}{2})x^{\frac{m}{2}}+O\left(x^{\frac{m}{2}-\frac{2}{(j+1)^{2}}+\epsilon}q^{\epsilon}\right)

and

(128)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)l_{\frac{m}{2}}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)

\displaystyle=O\left(x^{\frac{m}{2}-\frac{2}{(j+1)^{2}}+\epsilon}q^{1+\epsilon}\right),

where
\textstyle\chi
is not a principal Dirichlet character modulo
q
.

∎

Lemma 39
.

Let
j\geq 2
be a fixed integer. For
m=6,10
and for any fixed
\epsilon>0
and all sufficiently large

, we have

(129)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)r_{m}(n)(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)=\begin{cases}A\frac{\phi(q)}{q}x^{\frac{m}{2}}+O\left(x^{\frac{m}{2}-\frac{2}{(j+1)^{2}}+\epsilon}q^{1+\epsilon}\right),\quad\text{ when }{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}={\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}\text{ or }{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}={\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0},\\

Proof.

We estimate
\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)v_{3}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)
, where
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}={\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}
. Then, using the above procedure, we have

(130)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)v_{3}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)=\frac{\phi(4q)}{4q}b^{\prime\prime}_{j,f}(3)x^{3}+O\left(\frac{x^{2+\epsilon}}{T}q^{\epsilon}+x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}T^{\frac{(j+1)^{2}}{4}-1+\epsilon}q^{\epsilon}\right),

where

(131)

\displaystyle b^{\prime\prime}_{j,f}(3)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}L(3,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}})\prod_{n=1}^{j}L(3,\mathrm{sym}^{2n}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}})L(1,\mathrm{sym}^{2n}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}})H_{j}^{(3)}(3,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}})

and

(132)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)l_{3}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)=O\left(\frac{x^{2+\epsilon}}{T}q^{\epsilon}+x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}q^{{}^{\frac{(j+1)^{2}}{4}+\epsilon}}T^{\frac{(j+1)^{2}}{4}-1+\epsilon}\right).

(133)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)r_{6}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)=\frac{\phi(4q)}{4q}b^{\prime\prime}_{j,f}(3)x^{3}+O\left(\frac{x^{2+\epsilon}}{T}q^{\epsilon}+x^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}T^{\frac{(j+1)^{2}}{4}-1+\epsilon}q^{{}^{\frac{(j+1)^{2}}{4}+\epsilon}}\right).

, then we have

(134)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)r_{6}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)=\frac{\phi(4q)}{4q}b^{\prime\prime}_{j,f}(3)x^{3}+O\left(x^{3-\frac{2}{(j+1)^{2}}+\epsilon}q^{1+\epsilon}\right),

for
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}\neq{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}
.

We estimate
\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)l_{3}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}(n)
and we have as proceeding as above,

(135)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)r_{6}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}(n)=\frac{\phi(q)}{q}b^{\prime}_{j,f}(3)x^{3}+O\left(x^{3-\frac{2}{(j+1)^{2}}+\epsilon}q^{1+\epsilon}\right),

where

(136)

\displaystyle b^{\prime}_{j,f}(3)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}L(3,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})\prod_{n=1}^{j}L(3,\mathrm{sym}^{2n}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})L(1,\mathrm{sym}^{2n}f\otimes{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0})H_{j}(3,{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}).

For
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}\neq{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}
and
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}\neq{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}
, we have

(137)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)r_{6}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)=O\left(x^{3-\frac{2}{(j+1)^{2}}+\epsilon}q^{1+\epsilon}\right).

Similarly we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)r_{10}(n)(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}(n)=\frac{64}{5}\frac{\phi(q)}{q}b^{\prime}_{j,f}(5)x^{5}+O\left(x^{5-\frac{2}{(j+1)^{2}}+\epsilon}q^{1+\epsilon}\right),

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)r_{10}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)=\frac{4}{5}\frac{\phi(4q)}{4q}b^{\prime\prime}_{j,f}(5)x^{5}+O\left(x^{5-\frac{2}{(j+1)^{2}}+\epsilon}q^{1+\epsilon}\right),

where
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}={\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}
and

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)r_{10}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)=O\left(x^{5-\frac{2}{(j+1)^{2}}+\epsilon}q^{1+\epsilon}\right),

where
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}\neq{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}
.

3.
Proof of Theorem
1

Let
\textstyle\chi
be a Dirichlet character modulo
q
. By orthogonality, we have

(141)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq x+1\\
n\equiv 1~(\mathrm{mod}~q)\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n)l_{1}(n)=\frac{1}{\phi(q)}\sum_{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(q)}\sum_{n\leq x+1}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n)l_{1}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)

(142)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\phi(q)}\left\{\sum_{n\leq x+1}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n)l_{1}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}(n)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq x+1\\
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}={\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n)l_{1}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)\right.

(143)

\displaystyle+\left.\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(q)\\
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}},{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}\neq{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}\\
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}\text{ non primitive}\end{subarray}}\sum_{n\leq x+1}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n)l_{1}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(q)\\
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4},{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}\neq{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}\\
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}\text{ non primitive}\end{subarray}}\sum_{n\leq x+1}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n)l_{1}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)\right\}

(144)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\phi(q)}\left(\sum_{1}+\sum_{2}+\sum_{3}+\sum_{4}\right).

Now by the Lemma
35
, we have

(145)

\displaystyle\sum_{1}+\sum_{2}=O\left(x^{1-\frac{1}{j+1}+\epsilon}q^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\right)

and

(146)

\displaystyle\sum_{3}+\sum_{4}=O\left(x^{1-\frac{1}{j+1}+\epsilon}q^{1+\epsilon}\right).

Thus, we have

(147)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq x+1\\
n\equiv 1~(\mathrm{mod}~q)\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n)r_{2}(n)=O\left(\frac{x^{1-\frac{1}{j+1}+\epsilon}q^{1+\epsilon}}{\phi(q)}\right).

squares. By orthogonality, we have

\displaystyle+\left.\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(q)\\
