\displaystyle+\left.\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(q)\\
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}\neq{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}\\

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{4},{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}\neq{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{0}\\
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}\text{ non primitive}\end{subarray}}\sum_{n\leq x+1}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)r_{m}(n){\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}(n)\right\}

(171)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\phi(q)}\left(\sum_{1}+\sum_{2}+\sum_{3}+\sum_{4}\right).

39
, we have

(172)

\displaystyle\sum_{1}+\sum_{2}=A\frac{\phi(q)}{q}x^{\frac{m}{2}}+O\left(x^{\frac{m}{2}-\frac{2}{(j+1)^{2}}+\epsilon}q^{1+\epsilon}\right)

Thus, in both cases, we have

(174)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq x+1\\
n\equiv 1~(\mathrm{mod}~q)\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)r_{m}(n)=A\frac{1}{q}x^{\frac{m}{2}}+O\left(\frac{x^{\frac{m}{2}-\frac{2}{(j+1)^{2}}+\epsilon}q^{1+\epsilon}}{\phi(q)}\right).

7.
Proof of Theorem
5

By the definition of
k
-full kernel function
a(n)
, we can decompose
a(n)
uniquely as
a(n)=k(n)q(n)
with
(k(n),q(n))=1
, where
k(n)
is
k
-full and
q(n)
is
k
-free. Also note that

a(n)=a(k(n))\ll n^{\epsilon},\quad\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n)\ll n^{\epsilon},\quad r_{m}(n)\ll n^{\frac{m}{2}+\epsilon},

for all
\epsilon>0
. Here we will follow the procedure in
[
12
]
.

Let
1<H\leq x^{\frac{2}{j+3}}
, then

(175)

\displaystyle\sum_{n\leq x}a(n)\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n+1)r_{m}(n+1)

(176)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq x\\
k(n)\leq H\end{subarray}}a(n)\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n+1)r_{m}(n+1)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq x\\
k(n)>H\end{subarray}}a(n)\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n+1)r_{m}(n+1)

(177)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq x\\
k(n)\leq H\end{subarray}}a(n)\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n+1)r_{m}(n+1)+O\left(\sum_{H<k(n)\leq x}a(k(n))\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q(n)\leq\frac{x}{k(n)}\\
(q(n),k(n))=1\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n+1)r_{m}(n+1)\right)

(178)

\displaystyle=I_{1}+I_{2}.

Now

(179)

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle=O\left(\sum_{H<k(n)\leq x}(k(n))^{\epsilon}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q(n)\leq\frac{x}{k(n)}\\
(q(n),k(n))=1\end{subarray}}(k(n)q(n))^{\frac{m}{2}-1+\epsilon}\right)

(180)

\displaystyle=O\left(\sum_{H<k(n)\leq x}(k(n))^{\frac{m}{2}-1+2\epsilon}\left(\frac{x}{k(n)}\right)^{\frac{m}{2}+\epsilon}\right)=O\left(x^{5+\epsilon}\sum_{H<k(n)\leq x}\frac{1}{(k(n))^{1-\epsilon}}\right)

(181)

\displaystyle=O(x^{\frac{m}{2}+\epsilon}H^{\frac{1}{k}-1+\epsilon}).

Here we have used the fact that

\#\{n\leq H\bigg|\text{ }n\text{ is $k$ - full \lx@text@rbrace }\ll H^{1/k},\quad(\text{ by Lemma $2.12$ of \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Venkat}{}{}]} })

and

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n>H\\
n\text{ $k$-full}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{n^{1-\epsilon}}\ll H^{\frac{1}{k}-1+\epsilon},\quad(\text{ by Lemma $2.12$ of \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Venkat}{}{}]} }).

Now define
g(l)=\sum_{md^{k}=l}\mu(d)
, then
g(q(n)=1
as
q(n)
is
k
-free. We have

(182)

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq x\\
k(n)\leq H\end{subarray}}a(n)\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n+1)r_{m}(n+1)

(183)

\displaystyle\ll\sum_{k(n)\leq H}a(k(n))\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q(n)\leq\frac{x}{k(n)}\\
(q(n),k(n)=1\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(k(n)q(n)+1)r_{m}(k(n)q(n)+1)

(184)

\displaystyle\ll\sum_{k(n)\leq H}a(k(n))\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q(n)\leq\frac{x}{k(n)}\\
(q(n),k(n)=1\end{subarray}}g(q(n))\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(k(n)q(n)+1)r_{m}(k(n)q(n)+1)

(185)

\displaystyle\ll\sum_{k(n)\leq H}a(k(n))\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q(n)\leq\frac{x}{k(n)}\\
(q(n),k(n)=1\end{subarray}}\sum_{m(n)d^{k}(n)=q(n)}\mu(d(n))\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(k(n)q(n)+1)r_{m}(k(n)q(n)+1)

(186)

\displaystyle\ll\sum_{k(n)\leq H}a(k(n))\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d(n)\leq(\frac{x}{k(n)})^{1/k}\\
(d(n),k(n))=1\end{subarray}}\mu(d(n))\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m(n)\leq\frac{x}{k(n)d^{k}(n)}\\
(m(n),k(n)=1\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(k(n)m(n)d^{k}(n)+1)r_{m}(k(n)m(n)d^{k}(n)+1)

(187)

\displaystyle=\sum_{1}^{*}+\sum_{2}^{*},

where

(188)

\displaystyle\sum_{1}^{*}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k(n)\leq H}a(k(n))\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d(n)\leq H^{1/k}\\
(d(n),k(n))=1\end{subarray}}\mu(d(n))

(189)

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m(n)\leq\frac{x}{k(n)d^{k}(n)}\\
(m(n),k(n)=1\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(k(n)m(n)d^{k}(n)+1)r_{m}(k(n)m(n)d^{k}(n)+1)

and

(190)

\displaystyle\sum_{2}^{*}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k(n)\leq H}a(k(n))\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}H^{1/k}<d(n)\leq(\frac{x}{k(n)})^{1/k}\\
(d(n),k(n))=1\end{subarray}}\mu(d(n))

(191)

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m(n)\leq\frac{x}{k(n)d^{k}(n)}\\
(m(n),k(n)=1\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(k(n)m(n)d^{k}(n)+1)r_{m}(k(n)m(n)d^{k}(n)+1).

Now for
\sum_{2}^{*}
, we have

(192)

\displaystyle\sum_{2}^{*}

\displaystyle\ll\sum_{k(n)\leq H}(k(n))^{\epsilon}\sum_{d(n)\geq H^{1/k}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m(n)\leq\frac{x}{k(n)d^{k}(n)}\\
(m(n),k(n)=1\end{subarray}}(k(n)m(n)d^{k}(n))^{\frac{m}{2}-1+\epsilon}

(193)

\displaystyle\ll\sum_{k(n)\leq H}(k(n))^{\frac{m}{2}-1+2\epsilon}\sum_{d(n)\geq H^{1/k}}(d^{k}(n))^{\frac{m}{2}-1+\epsilon}\left(\frac{x}{k(n)d^{k}(n)}\right)^{\frac{m}{2}+\epsilon}

(194)

\displaystyle\ll x^{\frac{m}{2}+\epsilon}\sum_{k(n)\leq H}\frac{1}{(k(n))^{1-\epsilon}}\sum_{d^{k}(n)\geq H}\frac{1}{d^{k}(n)}

(195)

\displaystyle\ll x^{\frac{m}{2}+\epsilon}[H^{\epsilon}+\zeta(1-\epsilon)+O(1/H^{1-\epsilon})]H^{1/k-1}

(196)

\displaystyle\ll x^{\frac{m}{2}+\epsilon}H^{1/k-1+\epsilon}.

Now note that

\sum_{\delta(n)|(m(n),k(n))}\mu(\delta(n))=\begin{cases}1\text{ if }(m(n),k(n))=1,\\
0\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

So we have for
\sum_{1}^{*}
,

(197)

\displaystyle\sum_{1}^{*}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k(n)\leq H}a(k(n))\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d(n)\leq H^{1/k}\\
(d(n),k(n))=1\end{subarray}}\mu(d(n))\sum_{\delta(n)|k(n)}\mu(\delta(n))

(198)

\displaystyle\times\sum_{m_{1}(n)\delta(n)k(n)d^{k}(n)\leq x}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(k(n)m_{1}(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n)+1)r_{m}(k(n)m_{1}(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n)+1).

Here
m_{1}(n)
varies, so we can write,

(199)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}(n)\delta(n)k(n)d^{k}(n)\leq x\\
\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(~k(n)m_{1}(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n)+1~)r_{m}(~k(n)m_{1}(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n)+1~)

(200)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R\leq x+1\\
R\equiv 1~(\mathrm{mod}~k(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n))\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(R)r_{m}(R)

(201)

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{x^{\frac{m}{2}+\epsilon-\frac{2}{j+3}}(k(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n))^{\frac{j+1}{j+3}+\epsilon}}{\phi(k(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n))}\right)\quad\text{(by Theorem }~\ref{thm:main})

(202)

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{x^{\frac{m}{2}+\epsilon-\frac{2}{j+3}}(k(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n))^{1+\epsilon}}{\phi(k(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n))}\right).

So

(203)

\displaystyle\sum_{1}^{*}

\displaystyle\ll\sum_{k(n)\leq H}a(k(n))\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d(n)\leq H^{1/k}\\
(d(n),k(n))=1\end{subarray}}\mu(d(n))\sum_{\delta(n)|k(n)}\mu(\delta(n))\frac{x^{\frac{m}{2}+\epsilon-\frac{2}{j+3}}(k(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n))^{1+\epsilon}}{\phi(k(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n))}

(204)

\displaystyle\ll\sum_{k(n)\leq H}(k(n))^{\epsilon}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d(n)\leq H^{1/k}\\
(d(n),k(n))=1\end{subarray}}1\sum_{\delta(n)|k(n)}x^{\frac{m}{2}+\epsilon-\frac{2}{j+3}}(k(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n))^{\epsilon}\frac{k(n)\delta(n)}{\phi(k(n)\delta(n))}\frac{d^{k}(n)}{\phi(d^{k}(n))}

(205)

\displaystyle\ll x^{\frac{m}{2}+4\epsilon-\frac{2}{j+3}}\sum_{k(n)\leq H}(k(n))^{\epsilon}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d(n)\leq H^{1/k}\end{subarray}}1\sum_{\delta(n)|k(n)}\log\log(k(n)\delta(n))\log\log(d^{k}(n))

(206)

\displaystyle\ll x^{\frac{m}{2}+4\epsilon-\frac{2}{j+3}}\log\log H^{2}\log\log H\sum_{k(n)\leq H}(k(n))^{\epsilon}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d(n)\leq H^{1/k}\end{subarray}}1\sum_{\delta(n)|k(n)}1

(207)

\displaystyle\ll x^{\frac{m}{2}+4\epsilon-\frac{2}{j+3}}\log\log H^{2}\log\log H\sum_{k(n)\leq H}(k(n))^{2\epsilon}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d(n)\leq H^{1/k}\end{subarray}}1

(208)

\displaystyle\ll x^{\frac{m}{2}+4\epsilon-\frac{2}{j+3}}\log\log H^{2}\log\log H\sum_{k(n)\leq H}(k(n))^{2\epsilon}H^{1/k}

(209)

\displaystyle\ll x^{\frac{m}{2}+4\epsilon-\frac{2}{j+3}}H^{2\epsilon}H^{1/k}\log\log H^{2}\log\log H\sum_{k(n)\leq H}1

(210)

\displaystyle\ll x^{\frac{m}{2}+\epsilon-\frac{2}{j+3}}H^{2/k+\epsilon}.

Thus combining everything, we have

(211)

\displaystyle\sum_{n\leq x}a(n)\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n+1)r_{m}(n+1)=O\left(x^{\frac{m}{2}+\epsilon}H^{1/k-1+\epsilon}+x^{\frac{m}{2}-\frac{2}{j+3}+\epsilon}H^{2/k+\epsilon}\right).

Note that
q=\delta(n)k(n)d^{k}(n)
and
\delta(n),k(n),d^{k}(n)\leq H
, so
q\leq H^{3}
. Also
x
is a very large number, so we can consider
q\ll x^{\frac{2}{j+1}-\epsilon}
. Thus we choose a optimal value of
H
, which is
H=x^{\frac{2}{3(j+1)}-\epsilon}
, and we obtain

(212)

\displaystyle\sum_{n\leq x}a(n)\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n+1)r_{m}(n+1)

\displaystyle=O\left(x^{\frac{m}{2}-\frac{2k-2}{3k(j+1)}+\epsilon}+x^{\frac{m}{2}+\frac{4}{3k(j+1)}-\frac{2}{j+3}+\epsilon}\right)=O\left(x^{\frac{m}{2}-\frac{2k-2}{3k(j+1)}+\epsilon}\right),

which is true for
j\geq 1
. Thus we have our result for
m=4,6,8,10,12
.

Now for
m=2
, it will be same upto (
197
) and we have from (
199
),

(213)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}(n)\delta(n)k(n)d^{k}(n)\leq x\\
\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(k(n)m_{1}(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n)+1)r_{2}(k(n)m_{1}(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n)+1)

(214)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R\leq x+1\\
R\equiv 1~(\mathrm{mod~}k(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n))\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(R)r_{2}(R)

(215)

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{x^{1+\epsilon-\frac{1}{j+1}}(k(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n))^{1+\epsilon}}{\phi(k(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n))}\right)\quad\text{( by theorem }~\ref{thm:main}).

So as in the previous calculation, we have

(216)

\displaystyle\sum_{1}^{*}

\displaystyle\ll\sum_{k(n)\leq H}a(k(n))\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d(n)\leq H^{1/k}\\
(d(n),k(n))=1\end{subarray}}\mu(d(n))\sum_{\delta(n)|k(n)}\mu(\delta(n))\frac{x^{1+\epsilon-\frac{1}{j+1}}(k(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n))^{1+\epsilon}}{\phi(k(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n))}

(217)

\displaystyle\ll x^{1+\epsilon-\frac{1}{j+1}}H^{2/k+\epsilon}.

Thus, combining everything, we have

(218)

\displaystyle\sum_{n\leq x}a(n)\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n+1)r_{2}(n+1)=O\left(x^{1+\epsilon}H^{1/k-1+\epsilon}+x^{1-\frac{1}{j+1}+\epsilon}H^{2/k+\epsilon}\right).

As above, we note that
q\ll x^{\frac{1}{j+1}-\epsilon}
and we take
H=x^{\frac{1}{3(j+1)}-\epsilon}
. Thus, we have

(219)

\displaystyle\sum_{n\leq x}a(n)\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n+1)r_{2}(n+1)=O\left(x^{1-\frac{k-1}{3k(j+1)}+\epsilon}\right).

8.
Proof of Theorem
6

Note that
a^{2}(n)=a^{2}(k(n)
and
a^{2}(n)\ll n^{\epsilon}
for all
\epsilon>0
. Now to prove this theorem, we will follow the same steps up to (
197
) in the Theorem
5
. Then we have

(220)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}(n)\delta(n)k(n)d^{k}(n)\leq x\\
\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(k(n)m_{1}(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n)+1)r_{m}(k(n)m_{1}(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n)+1)

(221)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R\leq x+1\\
R\equiv 1~(\mathrm{mod~}k(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n))\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(R)r_{m}(R)

(222)

\displaystyle=\frac{c_{j,f}(\frac{m}{2})}{k(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n)}x^{\frac{m}{2}}+O\left(\frac{x^{\frac{m}{2}+\epsilon-\frac{2}{(j+1)^{2}}}(k(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n))^{1+\epsilon}}{\phi(k(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n))}\right),\quad(\text{ by Theorem~\ref{thm:main1}}).

So we have

(223)

\displaystyle\sum_{1}^{*}=\sum_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}+\sum_{1}^{"},

where

(224)

\displaystyle\sum_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}=\sum_{k(n)\leq H}a^{2}(k(n))\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d(n)\leq H^{1/k}\\
(d(n),k(n))=1\end{subarray}}\mu(d(n))\sum_{\delta(n)|k(n)}\mu(\delta(n))\frac{c_{j,f}(\frac{m}{2})x^{\frac{m}{2}}}{\delta(n)k(n)d^{k}(n)},

and proceeding as in the previous theorem, we have

(225)

\displaystyle\sum_{1}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\ll x^{\frac{m}{2}+\epsilon-\frac{2}{(j+1)^{2}}}H^{2/k+\epsilon}.

Now

(226)

\displaystyle\sum_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=c_{j,f}(\frac{m}{2})x^{\frac{m}{2}}\sum_{k(n)\leq H}\frac{a^{2}(k(n))}{k(n)}\sum_{\delta(n)|k(n)}\frac{\mu(\delta(n))}{\delta(n)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d(n)\leq H^{1/k}\\
(d(n),k(n))=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(d(n))}{d^{k}(n)}

(227)

\displaystyle=c_{j,f}(\frac{m}{2})x^{\frac{m}{2}}\sum_{k(n)=1}^{\infty}\frac{a^{2}(k(n))}{k(n)}\sum_{\delta(n)|k(n)}\frac{\mu(\delta(n))}{\delta(n)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d(n)=1\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\mu(d(n))}{d^{k}(n)}

(228)

\displaystyle+O\left(x^{\frac{m}{2}}\sum_{k(n)=1}^{\infty}\frac{a^{2}(k(n))}{k(n)}\sum_{\delta(n)|k(n)}\frac{\mu(\delta(n))}{\delta(n)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d(n)>H^{1/k}\\
(d(n),k(n))=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(d(n))}{d^{k}(n)}\right)

(229)

\displaystyle+O\left(x^{\frac{m}{2}}\sum_{k(n)>H}\frac{a^{2}(k(n))}{k(n)}\sum_{\delta(n)|k(n)}\frac{\mu(\delta(n))}{\delta(n)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d(n)\leq H^{1/k}\\
(d(n),k(n))=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(d(n))}{d^{k}(n)}\right)

(230)

\displaystyle=c_{j,f}(\frac{m}{2})x^{\frac{m}{2}}\sum_{k(n)=1}^{\infty}\frac{a^{2}(k(n))}{k(n)}\sum_{\delta(n)|k(n)}\frac{\mu(\delta(n))}{\delta(n)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d(n)=1\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\mu(d(n))}{d^{k}(n)}+J_{1}+J_{2}.

Now

(231)

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle\ll x^{\frac{m}{2}}\sum_{k(n)=1}^{\infty}\frac{(k(n))^{\epsilon}}{k(n)}\sum_{\delta(n)|k(n)}\frac{1}{\delta(n)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d(n)>H^{1/k}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{d^{k}(n)}

(232)

\displaystyle\ll x^{\frac{m}{2}}\sum_{k(n)=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k(n)^{1-\epsilon}}\sum_{\delta(n)|k(n)}1\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d^{k}(n)>H\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{d^{k}(n)}\ll x^{\frac{m}{2}}\sum_{k(n)=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k(n)^{1-2\epsilon}}H^{1/k-1}

(233)

\displaystyle\ll x^{\frac{m}{2}}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n^{k-2k\epsilon}}H^{1/k-1}\ll x^{\frac{m}{2}}H^{1/k-1}\quad(\text{ as }k(n)\text{ is $k$-full }).

Again

(234)

\displaystyle J_{2}\ll x^{\frac{m}{2}}\sum_{k(n)>H}\frac{1}{(k(n))^{1-\epsilon}}\sum_{\delta(n)|k(n)}1\sum_{d(n)\leq H^{1/k}}\frac{1}{d^{k}(n)}

(235)

\displaystyle\ll x^{\frac{m}{2}}H^{1/k-1+2\epsilon}\left(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n^{k}}+\sum_{d^{k}(n)>H}\frac{1}{d^{k}(n)}\right)\ll x^{\frac{m}{2}}H^{1/k-1+\epsilon}+x^{5}H^{2/k-2+2\epsilon}\ll x^{\frac{m}{2}}H^{1/k-1+\epsilon}.

Thus we have
J_{1}+J_{2}=O(x^{\frac{m}{2}+\epsilon}H^{1/k-1+\epsilon})
and the first term is finite. So

(236)

\displaystyle\sum_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}=C_{j,f}x^{\frac{m}{2}}+O(x^{\frac{m}{2}+\epsilon}H^{1/k-1+\epsilon}).

Thus, combining everything, we have

(237)

\displaystyle\sum_{n\leq x}a(n)\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n+1)r_{m}(n+1)

\displaystyle=C_{j,f}x^{\frac{m}{2}}+O(x^{\frac{m}{2}+\epsilon}H^{1/k-1+\epsilon}+x^{\frac{m}{2}+\epsilon-\frac{2}{(j+1)^{2}}}H^{2/k+\epsilon}).

we now choose
H=x^{\frac{2}{3(j+1)^{2}}-\epsilon}
and we obtain

(238)

\displaystyle\sum_{n\leq x}a(n)\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n+1)r_{m}(n+1)

\displaystyle=C_{j,f}x^{\frac{m}{2}}+O\left(x^{\frac{m}{2}-\frac{2k-2}{3(j+1)^{2}k}+\epsilon}\right).

So the result happens for
m=4,6,8
.

Now for
m=2
, we have from (
220
),

(239)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}(n)\delta(n)k(n)d^{k}(n)\leq x\\
\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(k(n)m_{1}(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n)+1)r_{2}(k(n)m_{1}(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n)+1)

(240)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R\leq x+1\\
R\equiv 1~(\mathrm{mod}~k(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n))\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(R)r_{2}(R)

(241)

\displaystyle=\frac{c_{j,f}(1)}{k(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n)}x+O\left(\frac{x^{1+\epsilon-\frac{1}{(j+1)^{2}}}(k(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n))^{1+\epsilon}}{\phi(k(n)\delta(n)d^{k}(n))}\right),\quad(\text{ by Theorem~\ref{thm:main1}}),

and proceeding as in the previous case, we have

(242)

\displaystyle\sum_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=D_{f,j}x+O\left(x^{1+\epsilon}H^{1/k-1+\epsilon}\right)

and

(243)

\displaystyle\sum_{1}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\ll x^{1-\frac{1}{(j+1)^{2}}+\epsilon}H^{2/k+\epsilon}

Thus, combining everything, we have

(244)

\displaystyle\sum_{n\leq x}a(n)\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n+1)r_{2}(n+1)

\displaystyle=C_{j,f}x+O(x^{1+\epsilon}H^{1/k-1+\epsilon}+x^{1+\epsilon-\frac{1}{(j+1)^{2}}}H^{2/k+\epsilon}).

we now choose
H=x^{\frac{2}{3(j+1)^{2}}-\epsilon}
and we obtain

(245)

\displaystyle\sum_{n\leq x}a(n)\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n+1)r_{2}(n+1)

\displaystyle=C_{j,f}x+O\left(x^{1-\frac{k-1}{3(j+1)^{2}k}+\epsilon}\right).

9.
Proof of Theorem
7

Let
S(x)=\sum_{n\leq x}a(n)\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n+1)r_{2}(n+1)
and
h=x^{\delta_{j}}
with
A(j)\coloneqq 1-\frac{k-1}{3k(j+1)^{2}}<\delta_{j}<1
. Now suppose that
\{a(n)\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n+1)|n=a_{1}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}-1,a_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}\}
does not change any sign in the interval
n\in(x,x+h]
and without loss of generality suppose that the sequence stays positive in the given interval.

Using the second result of Theorem
5
, we have

(246)

\displaystyle\sum_{x<n\leq x+h}a^{2}(n)\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n+1)r_{2}(n+1)

(247)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x<n\leq x+h}a(n)\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n+1)a(n)\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n+1)r_{2}(n+1)

(248)

\displaystyle\ll(x+h)^{\epsilon}\sum_{x<n\leq x+h}a(n)\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n+1)r_{2}(n+1)\quad(\text{as }a(n)\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n+1)\ll n^{\epsilon}\text{ for any }\epsilon>0)

(249)

\displaystyle\ll x^{\epsilon}(|S(x+h)|+|S(x)|)

(250)

\displaystyle\ll x^{1-\frac{k-1}{3k(j+1)}+\epsilon},

for any
\epsilon>0
, as small as possible.

Now using the second result of Theorem
6
, we have

(251)

\displaystyle\sum_{x<n\leq x+h}a^{2}(n)\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n+1)r_{2}(n+1)

\displaystyle=D_{j,f,2}h+O\left(x^{1-\frac{k-1}{3k(j+1)^{2}}+\epsilon}\right)

(252)

\displaystyle=D_{j,f,2}x^{\delta_{j}}+O\left(x^{1-\frac{k-1}{3k(j+1)^{2}}+\epsilon}\right).

Lemma
34
ensures that

(253)

\displaystyle\sum_{x<n\leq x+h}a^{2}(n)\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n+1)r_{2}(n+1)\gg x^{\delta_{j}}.

Combining (
246
) and (
253
), we obtain

x^{\delta_{j}}\ll x^{1-\frac{k-1}{3k(j+1)}+\epsilon}

as
x\to\infty
for any
\epsilon>0.
That is,
x^{\delta_{j}-A(j)-\epsilon}\ll 1
as
x\to\infty
for any
\epsilon>0.
In particular, choosing
\epsilon=\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{j}-A(j))>0
, we obtain that
x^{\epsilon}\ll 1
as
x\to\infty
, which is a contradiction.

This implies that there exists at least one sign change in the interval
(x,x+x^{\delta_{j}}]
, where
x
is sufficiently large. Similarly, we can prove that there exists at least one sign change in
(x+x^{\delta_{j}},x+2x^{\delta_{j}}]
and so on.

Note that
2x=x+x^{1-\delta_{j}}x^{\delta_{j}}
, and we have that there exists at least
x^{1-\delta_{j}}
number of sign changes in the interval
(x,2x]
.

10.
Proof of Theorem
8

Let
S_{m}(x)=\sum_{n\leq x}a(n)\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n+1)r_{m}(n+1)
and
h=x^{\delta_{j}}
with
1-\frac{2k-2}{3k(j+1)^{2}}<\delta_{j}<1-\frac{k-1}{3k(j+1)^{2}}
. Now suppose that
\{a(n)\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n+1)|n=\sum_{i=1}^{m}a_{i}^{2}-1,a_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}\}
does not change any sign in the interval
n\in(x,x+h]
and without loss of generality suppose that the sequence stays positive in the given interval.

Using the first result of Theorem
5
, we have

(254)

\displaystyle\sum_{x<n\leq x+h}a^{2}(n)\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n+1)r_{m}(n+1)

(255)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x<n\leq x+h}a(n)\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n+1)a(n)\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n+1)r_{m}(n+1)

(256)

\displaystyle\ll(x+h)^{\epsilon}\sum_{x<n\leq x+h}a(n)\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n+1)r_{m}(n+1)\quad(\text{ as }a(n)\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}(n+1)\ll n^{\epsilon},\text{ for all }\epsilon>0)

(257)

\displaystyle\ll x^{\epsilon}(|S_{m}(x+h)|+|S_{m}(x)|)

(258)

\displaystyle\ll x^{\frac{m}{2}-\frac{2k-2}{3k(j+1)}+\epsilon}.

Now using the first result of Theorem
6
, we have

(259)

\displaystyle\sum_{x<n\leq x+h}a(n)^{2}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n+1)r_{m}(n+1)

(260)

\displaystyle=D_{j,f,m}(x+h)^{\frac{m}{2}}-D_{j,f,m}x^{\frac{m}{2}}+O\left(x^{\frac{m}{2}-\frac{2}{(j+1)^{2}}+\epsilon}\right)

(261)

\displaystyle=D_{j,f,m}(c_{1}x^{\frac{m}{2}-1+\delta_{j}}+c_{2}x^{\frac{m}{2}-2+2\delta_{j}}+\cdots+c_{\frac{m}{2}}x^{\frac{m}{2}\delta_{j}})+O\left(x^{\frac{m}{2}-\frac{2}{(j+1)^{2}}+\epsilon}\right)

(262)

\displaystyle=D_{j,f,m}^{\prime}x^{\frac{m}{2}-1+\delta_{j}}+O\left(x^{\frac{m}{2}-\frac{2}{(j+1)^{2}}+\epsilon}\right)\quad\left(\text{ as }\delta_{j}<1-\frac{k-1}{(j+1)^{2}}\right),

where
D_{j,f,m}^{\prime}
is a constant, depending on
j,f
and
m
.

Thus, by the Lemma
34
, we have

(263)

\displaystyle\sum_{x<n\leq x+h}\lambda_{\mathrm{sym}^{j}f}^{2}(n)r_{m}(n)\gg x^{\frac{m}{2}-1+\delta_{j}}.

Combining (
254
) and (
263
), we have

(264)

\displaystyle x^{\frac{m}{2}-1+\delta_{j}}\ll x^{\frac{m}{2}-\frac{2k-2}{3k(j+1)}+\epsilon}.

This gives

(265)

\displaystyle x^{\frac{2k-2}{3k(j+1)}-\frac{2k-2}{3k(j+1)^{2}}+\epsilon}\leq x^{\frac{m}{2}-1+\delta_{j}-\frac{m}{2}+\frac{2}{j+1}+\epsilon}\ll 1,

for any
\epsilon>0
, as small as possible. This leads to a contradiction as
x\mapsto+\infty
.

This implies that there exists at least one sign change in the interval
(x,x+x^{\delta_{j}}]
, where
x
is sufficiently large. Similarly, we can prove that there exists at least one sign change in the interval
(x+x^{\delta_{j}},x+2x^{\delta_{j}}]
and so on. Thus, we have that there exists at least
x^{1-{\delta_{j}}}
number of sign changes in the interval
(x,2x]
.
